PHASE 7 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):

- Per-project content-addressable store at `<project_slug>/.cas/<sha256>` for produces artifacts.
- When a step's produces check accepts a file, the file is moved into `.cas/<hash>` and a symlink replaces the original path. Subsequent steps that produce identical content reuse the same CAS entry (no duplication).
- Per-project, NOT a shared CAS across projects (per SD: V1 stays single-host file-based with per-project scope).
- Files touched: `artagents/core/task/`, `artagents/core/project/run.py`.

EXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):
- Artifacts stored once and linked into step produces.
- No shared CAS exists across projects.
- Symlinks resolve to `.cas/<sha256>`.
- Identical content from different steps shares one CAS entry.

WHAT TO IMPLEMENT:

1. New module `artagents/core/task/cas.py` (or similar) with:
   - `cas_path(project_dir, sha256) -> Path` — returns `<project_dir>/.cas/<sha256>`
   - `intern(project_dir, source_path) -> Path` — moves file into CAS by content hash, returns CAS path. Idempotent: if hash already exists, leaves the existing entry and discards the source.
   - `link_into_produces(cas_path, target_path)` — symlinks `target_path -> cas_path` (relative symlink so the run dir stays portable).

2. Hook the produces flow (in the runner that handles produces checks):
   - After a produces check passes for a file artifact, intern the file and replace the original path with a symlink to the CAS entry.
   - Update `events.jsonl` records to include the CAS hash for each interned artifact.

3. Tests:
   - `tests/test_cas_intern.py`: writes a file, calls intern twice with identical content, asserts only one CAS entry exists and the second call discards the duplicate source.
   - `tests/test_cas_symlink.py`: full produces flow — step writes a file, post-check it's a symlink into `.cas/<hash>`.
   - `tests/test_cas_per_project.py`: two project slugs with identical content produce two separate CAS entries (per-project scope, NOT shared).

4. Update `artagents/structure.py` to acknowledge `.cas/` as a known per-project directory if there's a directory whitelist; otherwise leave structure alone.

5. Update `.gitignore` to exclude `*/runs/*/.cas/` and `**/.cas/` if not already.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Stay within Phase 7 scope. Do NOT touch Phase 8 inbox or Phase 9 golden tests.
- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.
- No new dependencies (use stdlib `hashlib` and `os.symlink`).
- Honor existing patterns: hash-chained events, gate above dispatch, file-based state.
- The CAS is per-project. There is no shared CAS across projects. Reject any design that introduces one.

STOP CONDITION: Phase 7 done when `pytest tests/` passes with new tests + the produces flow uses `.cas/<sha256>` symlinks.

# Implementation Plan: Phase 7 — Per-Project CAS for Produces Artifacts

## Overview
Phase 7 adds a per-project content-addressable store (CAS) at `<project_dir>/.cas/<sha256>` and rewrites every produces-checked file artifact to a relative symlink that points at its CAS entry. The CAS is **per-project, never shared** (SD-008, SD-029). Implementation is additive: a new `artagents/core/task/cas.py` module supplies `intern` / `link_into_produces` helpers, and a single hook inside `gate._run_inline_checks` interns each artifact after its check passes and emits the `cas_hash` on the existing `produces_check_passed` event. `artagents/core/project/run.py` already mirrors hype artifacts under `produces/` (line 160) — the CAS hook runs at the gate layer, so the same flow naturally feeds it for both code-step and attested produces.

Critical constraints:
- Stdlib only: `hashlib.sha256`, `os.symlink`, `os.replace`.
- Per-project scope: `cas_path` derives from `project_dir` (i.e. `<projects_root>/<slug>/.cas`) — the helper does NOT accept a `projects_root` and there is no top-level `.cas`. Two projects with identical content produce two separate CAS entries.
- Relative symlinks (computed via `os.path.relpath`) so the run dir stays portable when `<projects_root>` moves.
- `intern` is idempotent: if `<project_dir>/.cas/<sha256>` already exists, it discards the source rather than overwriting; subsequent identical content reuses the same entry.
- Symlinks already at the target path (e.g. on retry / reentry) are not re-walked.
- The hook lives **after** `entry.check.run(artifact_path)` returns ok and **before** the `produces_check_passed` event is appended, so the event records the CAS hash atomically with the pass.

Existing tests (`tests/test_task_inline_checks.py:113` `test_code_produces_check_passes_advances`) write a real file and currently assert it remains a regular file by implication only (they re-read no content). After Phase 7 that file becomes a symlink — `Path.read_text` follows symlinks so the existing assertion `kinds == [..., "produces_check_passed"]` keeps passing. No other test depends on the artifact being a non-symlink.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Create the CAS module (`artagents/core/task/cas.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a new file `artagents/core/task/cas.py` exposing three functions and the constant:
   - `CAS_DIRNAME = ".cas"`
   - `cas_path(project_dir: Path, sha256: str) -> Path` — returns `project_dir / CAS_DIRNAME / sha256`. Pure pathjoin; no I/O.
   - `hash_file(path: Path) -> str` — streams the file in 1 MiB chunks through `hashlib.sha256`, returns the hex digest.
   - `intern(project_dir: Path, source_path: Path) -> tuple[Path, str]` — returns `(cas_path, sha256)`. Behavior:
     - If `source_path` is already a symlink whose resolved target sits inside `project_dir / CAS_DIRNAME`, short-circuit: read the existing CAS hash from the link target's filename and return `(link_target, hash)` without touching anything.
     - Otherwise compute `digest = hash_file(source_path)`, `target = cas_path(project_dir, digest)`.
     - `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` on `target.parent`.
     - If `target.exists()`, treat as duplicate content: `source_path.unlink()` (the source is being replaced by a symlink in the next step anyway). Return `(target, digest)`.
     - Else `os.replace(source_path, target)` — atomic on the same filesystem; the projects root is single-host single-volume by design (SD-029).
     - Return `(target, digest)`.
   - `link_into_produces(cas_entry: Path, target_path: Path) -> None`:
     - If `target_path` exists or is a symlink, `target_path.unlink()` first.
     - Compute `rel = os.path.relpath(cas_entry, target_path.parent)` so the link stays portable.
     - `os.symlink(rel, target_path)`.
2. **Keep** the module under ~80 lines; pure stdlib; no logging, no tracing.
3. **Export** `intern`, `link_into_produces`, `cas_path`, `CAS_DIRNAME` via `__all__`.

### Step 2: Wire CAS into the produces-check pass path (`artagents/core/task/gate.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Import** `from artagents.core.task.cas import intern, link_into_produces` near the top of `gate.py`.
2. **Modify** `_run_inline_checks` (`artagents/core/task/gate.py:1110`) so that after `result.ok` is true, before the `make_produces_check_passed_event` append:
   ```python
   cas_hash = None
   if artifact_path.is_file() or artifact_path.is_symlink():
       cas_entry, cas_hash = intern(decision.project_root, artifact_path)
       link_into_produces(cas_entry, artifact_path)
   ```
   `decision.project_root` is `<projects_root>/<slug>` (already populated; see `_run_inline_checks` line 1119 for the parent-walk that already uses it).
3. **Pass** `cas_hash` through to the event by extending `make_produces_check_passed_event` (Step 3).
4. **Skip** interning when `artifact_path` is a directory or missing — the check already failed (we only run the intern when the file is a regular file or pre-existing symlink). `Path.is_file()` follows symlinks, so re-running over an already-interned symlink is a no-op via the short-circuit in `intern`.

### Step 3: Surface `cas_hash` on the produces_check_passed event (`artagents/core/task/events.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Update** `make_produces_check_passed_event` (`artagents/core/task/events.py:185`) to accept an optional `cas_hash: str | None = None` keyword. When non-None, include it in the returned dict as `"cas_hash": cas_hash` (placed alphabetically between `check_id` and `kind` for canonical-JSON stability).
2. **Update** the call site in `gate._run_inline_checks` to pass `cas_hash=cas_hash`.
3. **Verify** `canonical_event_json` already sorts keys (it does — used by hash chain). The new optional key is omitted when `cas_hash is None`, so legacy events keep their existing chain hash.

### Step 4: Acknowledge `.cas/` in repo guardrails (`artagents/structure.py`, `.gitignore`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** `artagents/structure.py:14` — `TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS` lives under `artagents/` and does NOT govern `<projects_root>/<slug>/`. Project directories live outside the repo (`~/Documents/reigh-workspace/artagents-projects/<slug>/`). No `structure.py` change is required; the brief explicitly says "otherwise leave structure alone."
2. **Append** to `.gitignore`:
   ```
   # Per-project CAS (Phase 7)
   **/.cas/
   ```
   `runs/` is already ignored (line ~30) so the per-run-tree case is covered, but `**/.cas/` is the documented guardrail for any in-repo test fixture that creates a project dir.

### Step 5: Add CAS unit tests (`tests/test_cas_intern.py`, `tests/test_cas_symlink.py`, `tests/test_cas_per_project.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`tests/test_cas_intern.py`** — exercises `intern` directly:
   - `test_intern_moves_file_into_cas`: writes `data.bin` with bytes `b"hello"`, calls `intern(project_dir, source)`, asserts the source is gone, the CAS entry exists at `project_dir/.cas/<sha256("hello")>`, and the returned hash matches.
   - `test_intern_idempotent_discards_duplicate_source`: writes the same content twice (different source paths), calls `intern` on each. Asserts the CAS dir contains exactly one file, both calls returned the same `(target, hash)`, and the second source path was unlinked.
   - `test_intern_short_circuits_on_existing_cas_symlink`: pre-creates a CAS entry, builds a relative symlink to it via `link_into_produces`, calls `intern` on the symlink. Asserts no new CAS entry is created and the returned hash matches.
2. **`tests/test_cas_symlink.py`** — full produces flow through the gate. Mirror the harness in `tests/test_task_inline_checks.py:113`:
   - Author a one-step plan with a `json_file` produces check, run `gate.gate_command`, write `out.json`, call `record_dispatch_complete`.
   - Assert `step_dir/out.json` is a symlink (`Path.is_symlink()`), `os.readlink` returns a relative path containing `/.cas/`, the resolved target sits under `<project_dir>/.cas/<sha256>`, and `out.json` still reads back as the original JSON content.
   - Assert the `produces_check_passed` event in `events.jsonl` carries a `cas_hash` field equal to the link target filename.
3. **`tests/test_cas_per_project.py`** — per-project scope:
   - Create two projects (`alpha`, `beta`) under the same `tmp_projects_root`.
   - Run identical produces flow in both: each writes the same byte content.
   - Assert `<root>/alpha/.cas/<hash>` AND `<root>/beta/.cas/<hash>` both exist as separate inodes (`Path.stat().st_ino` differs).
   - Assert no `<root>/.cas/` directory exists (no shared CAS leaked).

### Step 6: Run the test suite
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_cas_intern.py tests/test_cas_symlink.py tests/test_cas_per_project.py -x -q` first (cheap, targets new code).
2. **Run** `pytest tests/test_task_inline_checks.py tests/test_task_kernel_gate.py tests/test_project_runs.py -q` to catch regressions in the immediate Phase 1–3 surface.
3. **Run** `pytest tests/ -q` for the full additive guarantee. Phase 7 must keep every prior test green.

## Execution Order
1. Land `cas.py` (Step 1) and the optional `cas_hash` plumbing on the event factory (Step 3) before touching the gate — both are pure additions, no behavior change yet.
2. Wire the gate hook (Step 2) — this flips the produces-flow from regular files to symlinks.
3. Add the gitignore entry (Step 4) before running tests, so any in-repo `.cas/` fixtures stay un-staged.
4. Add tests (Step 5).
5. Validate (Step 6).

## Validation Order
1. Targeted CAS tests first (`test_cas_intern`, `test_cas_symlink`, `test_cas_per_project`).
2. Phase 1–3 regression sweep (`test_task_kernel_gate`, `test_task_inline_checks`, `test_project_runs`).
3. Full `pytest tests/`.

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"

  ],
  "is-the-scope-and-scale-of-the-change-appropriate": [

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

  {
    "subsystem": "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements",
    "total_occurrences": 3,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-145",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },
  {
    "subsystem": "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure",
    "total_occurrences": 3,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-014",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },

        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.

Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

1205	def command_for_argv(argv: Sequence[str]) -> str:
1206	    tokens = [str(token) for token in argv]
1207	    if len(tokens) >= 3 and Path(tokens[0]).name.startswith("python") and tokens[1:3] == ["-m", "artagents"]:
1208	        tokens = tokens[3:]
1209	    elif tokens and Path(tokens[0]).name.endswith("artagents"):
1210	        tokens = tokens[1:]
1211	    return " ".join(shlex.quote(token) for token in tokens)

1214	def _compute_inline_plan_hash(plan: TaskPlan) -> str:
1215	    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical_event_json(plan.to_dict()).encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
1216	    return f"sha256:{digest}"
1217	
1218	
1219	def _find_run_started_actor(events: Sequence[dict[str, Any]]) -> str | None:
1220	    for event in events:
1221	        if event.get("kind") == "run_started":
1222	            actor = event.get("actor")
1223	            return actor if isinstance(actor, str) else None
1224	    return None
1225

1227	def _reject(slug: str, reason: str, *, abort: bool) -> None:
1228	    verb = "abort" if abort else "next"
1229	    raise TaskRunGateError(reason=reason, recovery=f"artagents {verb} --project {slug}")

16	TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_FILES = {
17	    "__init__.py",
18	    "__main__.py",
19	    "_paths.py",
20	    "doctor.py",
21	    "pipeline.py",
22	    "setup_cli.py",
23	    "structure.py",
24	    "theme_schema.py",
25	    "timeline.py",
26	}
27	TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS = {
28	    "__pycache__",
29	    "audit",
30	    "contracts",
31	    "core",
32	    "domains",
33	    "elements",
34	    "modalities",
35	    "orchestrate",

43	@dataclass(frozen=True)
44	class StructureReport:
45	    errors: tuple[str, ...]
46	
47	    @property
48	    def ok(self) -> bool:
49	        return not self.errors
50	
51	
52	def validate_repo_structure(root: str | Path = REPO_ROOT) -> StructureReport:
53	    repo_root = Path(root)
54	    errors: list[str] = []
55	    errors.extend(_validate_legacy_dirs(repo_root))
56	    errors.extend(_validate_local_state_dirs(repo_root))
57	    errors.extend(_validate_top_level_artagents(repo_root / "artagents"))
58	    errors.extend(_validate_pack_executor_folders(repo_root / "artagents" / "packs"))
59	    errors.extend(_validate_pack_orchestrator_folders(repo_root / "artagents" / "packs"))
60	    errors.extend(_validate_pack_element_folders(repo_root / "artagents" / "packs"))
61	    return StructureReport(errors=tuple(errors))
62	
63	
64	def _validate_legacy_dirs(repo_root: Path) -> list[str]:
65	    errors: list[str] = []
66	    for dirname in LEGACY_PUBLIC_DIRS:
67	        candidate = repo_root / "artagents" / dirname
68	        if candidate.exists():
69	            errors.append(f"legacy public package must not exist: {candidate.relative_to(repo_root)}")
70	    return errors
71	
72	
73	def _validate_local_state_dirs(repo_root: Path) -> list[str]:
74	    errors: list[str] = []
75	    local_root = repo_root / ".artagents"
76	    if not local_root.exists():
77	        return errors
78	    for dirname in LEGACY_LOCAL_DIRS:
79	        candidate = local_root / dirname
80	        if candidate.exists():
81	            errors.append(f"legacy local state directory must not exist: {candidate.relative_to(repo_root)}")
82	    return errors
83	
84	
85	def _validate_top_level_artagents(package_root: Path) -> list[str]:
86	    errors: list[str] = []
87	    for child in sorted(package_root.iterdir()):
88	        if child.name.startswith("."):
89	            continue
90	        if child.is_file() and child.suffix == ".py" and child.name not in TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_FILES:
91	            errors.append(f"top-level artagents module must move to a canonical package: {child.relative_to(package_root.parents[0])}")
92	        if child.is_dir() and child.name not in TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS:
93	            errors.append(f"top-level artagents directory is not a canonical concept: {child.relative_to(package_root.parents[0])}")
94	    return errors
95	
96	
97	def _validate_pack_executor_folders(packs_root: Path) -> list[str]:
98	    if not packs_root.is_dir():
99	        return [f"missing packs directory: {packs_root}"]
100	
101	    errors: list[str] = []
102	    repo_root = packs_root.parents[1]
103	    for pack_dir in _public_child_dirs(packs_root, INTERNAL_PACK_DIRS):
104	        for folder in _public_child_dirs(pack_dir, INTERNAL_PACK_DIRS):
105	            if not _has_any(folder, ("executor.yaml", "executor.yml", "executor.json", "executor.py")):
106	                continue
107	            errors.extend(_require_files(folder, ("executor.yaml", "run.py", "STAGE.md"), root=repo_root))
108	            if _has_any(folder, ("orchestrator.yaml", "orchestrator.yml", "orchestrator.json", "orchestrator.py")):
109	                errors.append(f"executor folder contains orchestrator metadata: {folder.relative_to(repo_root)}")
110	            try:
111	                definitions = load_folder_executors(folder)
112	            except Exception as exc:
113	                errors.append(f"invalid executor folder {folder.relative_to(repo_root)}: {exc}")
114	                continue
115	            if not definitions:
116	                errors.append(f"executor folder emitted no executor metadata: {folder.relative_to(repo_root)}")
117	                continue
118	            for definition in definitions:
119	                pack_segment = definition.id.split(".", 1)[0]
120	                if pack_segment != pack_dir.name:
121	                    errors.append(
122	                        f"executor {definition.id!r} must live in pack {pack_segment!r} but was found in pack {pack_dir.name!r}"
123	                    )
124	    return errors
125	
126	
127	def _validate_pack_orchestrator_folders(packs_root: Path) -> list[str]:
128	    if not packs_root.is_dir():
129	        return [f"missing packs directory: {packs_root}"]
130	
131	    errors: list[str] = []
132	    repo_root = packs_root.parents[1]
133	    for pack_dir in _public_child_dirs(packs_root, INTERNAL_PACK_DIRS):
134	        for folder in _public_child_dirs(pack_dir, INTERNAL_PACK_DIRS):
135	            if not _has_any(folder, ("orchestrator.yaml", "orchestrator.yml", "orchestrator.json", "orchestrator.py")):
136	                continue
137	            errors.extend(_require_files(folder, ("orchestrator.yaml", "run.py", "STAGE.md"), root=repo_root))
138	            if _has_any(folder, ("executor.yaml", "executor.yml", "executor.json", "executor.py")):
139	                errors.append(f"orchestrator folder contains executor metadata: {folder.relative_to(repo_root)}")
140	            try:
141	                definitions = load_folder_orchestrators(folder)
142	            except Exception as exc:
143	                errors.append(f"invalid orchestrator folder {folder.relative_to(repo_root)}: {exc}")
144	                continue
145	            if not definitions:
146	                errors.append(f"orchestrator folder emitted no orchestrator metadata: {folder.relative_to(repo_root)}")
147	                continue
148	            for definition in definitions:
149	                pack_segment = definition.id.split(".", 1)[0]
150	                if pack_segment != pack_dir.name:
151	                    errors.append(
152	                        f"orchestrator {definition.id!r} must live in pack {pack_segment!r} but was found in pack {pack_dir.name!r}"
153	                    )
154	    return errors
155	
156	
157	_ELEMENT_KINDS = ("effects", "animations", "transitions")
158	
159	
160	def _validate_pack_element_folders(packs_root: Path) -> list[str]:
161	    if not packs_root.is_dir():
162	        return []
163	
164	    errors: list[str] = []
165	    repo_root = packs_root.parents[1]
166	    for pack_dir in _public_child_dirs(packs_root, INTERNAL_PACK_DIRS):
167	        elements_root = pack_dir / "elements"
168	        if not elements_root.is_dir():
169	            continue
170	        for kind_dir in _public_child_dirs(elements_root, INTERNAL_PACK_DIRS):
171	            if kind_dir.name not in _ELEMENT_KINDS:
172	                errors.append(
173	                    f"unexpected element kind folder {kind_dir.relative_to(repo_root)}: must be one of {list(_ELEMENT_KINDS)}"
174	                )
175	                continue
176	            for element_dir in _public_child_dirs(kind_dir, INTERNAL_PACK_DIRS):
177	                errors.extend(_require_files(element_dir, ("component.tsx", "element.yaml"), root=repo_root))
178	    return errors
179	
180	
181	def _public_child_dirs(root: Path, skipped: set[str]) -> tuple[Path, ...]:
182	    return tuple(sorted(path for path in root.iterdir() if path.is_dir() and path.name not in skipped and not path.name.startswith(".")))
183	
184	
185	def _require_files(folder: Path, filenames: tuple[str, ...], *, root: Path) -> list[str]:
186	    return [f"{folder.relative_to(root)} missing {filename}" for filename in filenames if not (folder / filename).is_file()]
187	
188	
189	def _has_any(folder: Path, filenames: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
190	    return any((folder / filename).exists() for filename in filenames)
191	
192	
193	__all__ = ["StructureReport", "validate_repo_structure"]
194

235	
236	def mirror_hype_artifacts(
237	    run_root: str | Path,
238	    *,
239	    brief_slug: str | None = None,
240	    artifact_roots: Iterable[str | Path] = (),
241	    dest_root: str | Path | None = None,
242	) -> dict[str, Any]:
243	    run_path = Path(run_root).expanduser().resolve()
244	    source = discover_hype_artifact_root(run_path, brief_slug=brief_slug, artifact_roots=artifact_roots)
245	    if source is None:
246	        return {}
247	    dest_path_root = Path(dest_root).expanduser().resolve() if dest_root is not None else run_path
248	    dest_path_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
249	    mirrored: dict[str, Any] = {}
250	    for key, (source_name, dest_name) in HYPE_ARTIFACTS.items():
251	        source_path = source / source_name
252	        dest_path = dest_path_root / dest_name
253	        shutil.copy2(source_path, dest_path)
254	        mirrored[key] = {"path": str(dest_path), "source_path": str(source_path)}
255	    return mirrored
256	
257	
258	def discover_hype_artifact_root(
259	    run_root: str | Path,
260	    *,
261	    brief_slug: str | None = None,
262	    artifact_roots: Iterable[str | Path] = (),
263	) -> Path | None:
264	    run_path = Path(run_root).expanduser().resolve()
265	    candidates = [Path(item).expanduser().resolve() for item in artifact_roots]
266	    candidates.append(run_path)
267	    if brief_slug:
268	        candidates.append(run_path / "briefs" / brief_slug)
269	    for candidate in candidates:
270	        if _has_hype_artifact_set(candidate):
271	            return candidate
272	    briefs_root = run_path / "briefs"
273	    if not briefs_root.is_dir():
274	        return None
275	    matches = sorted(path for path in briefs_root.iterdir() if path.is_dir() and _has_hype_artifact_set(path))
276	    if len(matches) > 1:
277	        raise ProjectRunError(
278	            "multiple nested hype artifact sets found; pass brief_slug so ArtAgents can choose one deterministically"
279	        )

        Idea:
Implement Phase 7 (Per-project CAS) of the ArtAgents orchestrator V1 design per docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md section 12 (Phase 7).

LAUNCHER: every `megaplan` call = `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan ...`.

DRIVING DISCIPLINE: After every CLI call, run `megaplan status --plan <name>` and `megaplan progress --plan <name>`. If `state == finalized` and `batches_remaining > 0`, you MUST run `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --batch N` for each remaining batch sequentially. DO NOT EXIT while state != done.


PHASE 7 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):

- Per-project content-addressable store at `<project_slug>/.cas/<sha256>` for produces artifacts.
- When a step's produces check accepts a file, the file is moved into `.cas/<hash>` and a symlink replaces the original path. Subsequent steps that produce identical content reuse the same CAS entry (no duplication).
- Per-project, NOT a shared CAS across projects (per SD: V1 stays single-host file-based with per-project scope).
- Files touched: `artagents/core/task/`, `artagents/core/project/run.py`.

EXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):
- Artifacts stored once and linked into step produces.
- No shared CAS exists across projects.
- Symlinks resolve to `.cas/<sha256>`.
- Identical content from different steps shares one CAS entry.

WHAT TO IMPLEMENT:

1. New module `artagents/core/task/cas.py` (or similar) with:
   - `cas_path(project_dir, sha256) -> Path` — returns `<project_dir>/.cas/<sha256>`
   - `intern(project_dir, source_path) -> Path` — moves file into CAS by content hash, returns CAS path. Idempotent: if hash already exists, leaves the existing entry and discards the source.
   - `link_into_produces(cas_path, target_path)` — symlinks `target_path -> cas_path` (relative symlink so the run dir stays portable).

2. Hook the produces flow (in the runner that handles produces checks):
   - After a produces check passes for a file artifact, intern the file and replace the original path with a symlink to the CAS entry.
   - Update `events.jsonl` records to include the CAS hash for each interned artifact.

3. Tests:
   - `tests/test_cas_intern.py`: writes a file, calls intern twice with identical content, asserts only one CAS entry exists and the second call discards the duplicate source.
   - `tests/test_cas_symlink.py`: full produces flow — step writes a file, post-check it's a symlink into `.cas/<hash>`.
   - `tests/test_cas_per_project.py`: two project slugs with identical content produce two separate CAS entries (per-project scope, NOT shared).

4. Update `artagents/structure.py` to acknowledge `.cas/` as a known per-project directory if there's a directory whitelist; otherwise leave structure alone.

5. Update `.gitignore` to exclude `*/runs/*/.cas/` and `**/.cas/` if not already.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Stay within Phase 7 scope. Do NOT touch Phase 8 inbox or Phase 9 golden tests.
- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.
- No new dependencies (use stdlib `hashlib` and `os.symlink`).
- Honor existing patterns: hash-chained events, gate above dispatch, file-based state.
- The CAS is per-project. There is no shared CAS across projects. Reject any design that introduces one.

STOP CONDITION: Phase 7 done when `pytest tests/` passes with new tests + the produces flow uses `.cas/<sha256>` symlinks.

        Plan:

# Implementation Plan: Phase 7 — Per-Project CAS for Produces Artifacts

## Overview
Phase 7 adds a per-project content-addressable store at `<project_dir>/.cas/<sha256>` and rewrites every produces-checked file artifact into a relative symlink that points at its CAS entry. The CAS is **per-project, never shared** (SD-008, SD-029). A new `artagents/core/task/cas.py` module supplies `intern` / `link_into_produces` helpers; one hook inside `gate._run_inline_checks` interns each artifact after its check passes and records the `cas_hash` on the existing `produces_check_passed` event. One small defensive edit in `artagents/core/project/run.py` (the file the brief explicitly lists as touched) keeps the hype-artifact mirror compatible with CAS by preserving symlinks rather than dereferencing them.

Critical constraints:
- Stdlib only: `hashlib.sha256`, `os.symlink`, `os.replace`, `shutil.copyfile`.
- Per-project scope: `cas_path(project_dir, sha256)` lives at `<projects_root>/<slug>/.cas/<sha256>`. No top-level / cross-project CAS exists.
- Relative symlinks via `os.path.relpath` so the project dir stays portable across `<projects_root>` relocations.
- `intern` is idempotent and **always stores regular file content** in `.cas/<sha>`, never a symlink — even when the source is itself a non-CAS symlink (correctness flag fix below).
- The hook lives **after** `entry.check.run(artifact_path)` returns ok and **before** the `produces_check_passed` event is appended, so the event records the CAS hash atomically with the pass.
- `cas_hash` is the **bare hex digest** (matches the on-disk filename and lets event consumers locate the CAS entry by direct string concatenation). This deliberately diverges from `plan_hash`'s `sha256:<hex>` form, because `plan_hash` is an opaque identifier while `cas_hash` is a filesystem path component (Settled: see SD-P7-cas-hash-format below).

### Resolution of unresolved flags
- **FLAG-P7-001 / issue_hints-1 (`run.py` divergence).** The gate IS the single chokepoint for produces interning; `mirror_hype_artifacts` runs upstream of `_run_inline_checks` (the parent task gate's produces check fires AFTER the mirror has copied the hype output to `<step_dir>/produces/<name>`), so the mirrored file is interned naturally. **However**, if the hype-side source is already a CAS symlink (from an earlier interning pass), `shutil.copy2(source, dest)` defaults to `follow_symlinks=True` and dereferences it into a fresh regular file — wasting an intern cycle and briefly doubling storage. Step 4 below adds `follow_symlinks=False` to that single `shutil.copy2` call, so the dest becomes a symlink replica that `intern`'s short-circuit immediately recognizes. This is the minimum touch in `run.py` that the brief listing requires.
- **issue_hints-2 (`cas_hash` format).** Settled as **bare hex**. Rationale recorded in Overview and as SD-P7-cas-hash-format.
- **correctness (symlink source).** `intern` is rewritten to never `os.replace` a symlink. When the source is a symlink, it `shutil.copyfile`'s the dereferenced bytes into the CAS target and then unlinks the source — guaranteeing the CAS entry is always a regular file.
- **all_locations (gitignore).** The misleading "runs/ already covers per-run-tree" comment is removed; the new gitignore entry stands on its own merits as the in-repo-test-fixture guardrail.

Existing tests (`tests/test_task_inline_checks.py:113`) write a real file and re-read it via JSON-check helpers; both `Path.is_file` and the JSON read follow symlinks, so those tests keep passing after the flip.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Create the CAS module (`artagents/core/task/cas.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a new file `artagents/core/task/cas.py` exposing:
   - `CAS_DIRNAME = ".cas"`
   - `cas_path(project_dir: Path, sha256: str) -> Path` — pure pathjoin: `project_dir / CAS_DIRNAME / sha256`. No I/O.
   - `hash_file(path: Path) -> str` — streams `path` (open with default symlink-following) in 1 MiB chunks through `hashlib.sha256`; returns the hex digest.
   - `intern(project_dir: Path, source_path: Path) -> tuple[Path, str]` — guaranteed to leave a regular file in CAS:
     - If `source_path.is_symlink()`:
       - Read its real target via `source_path.resolve(strict=True)`.
       - If the resolved target sits inside `project_dir / CAS_DIRNAME`, short-circuit: extract the existing hash from the resolved filename (`resolved.name`) and return `(resolved, hash)` without any writes or unlinks.
       - Otherwise: compute `digest = hash_file(source_path)`, `target = cas_path(project_dir, digest)`, `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` on `target.parent`. If `target.exists()`, just `source_path.unlink()`; else `shutil.copyfile(source_path, target)` then `source_path.unlink()`. Return `(target, digest)`. (`copyfile` with default args follows symlinks, so the CAS entry holds dereferenced bytes.)
     - Else (regular file): compute `digest = hash_file(source_path)`, `target = cas_path(project_dir, digest)`, `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` on `target.parent`. If `target.exists()`, `source_path.unlink()` (duplicate content). Else `os.replace(source_path, target)` (atomic same-filesystem rename). Return `(target, digest)`.
   - `link_into_produces(cas_entry: Path, target_path: Path) -> None`:
     - If `target_path.exists()` or `target_path.is_symlink()`, `target_path.unlink()` first.
     - `rel = os.path.relpath(cas_entry, target_path.parent)`; `os.symlink(rel, target_path)`.
2. **Keep** the module under ~80 lines, stdlib-only (`hashlib`, `os`, `shutil`, `pathlib`), with no logging or fallback machinery.
3. **Export** `intern`, `link_into_produces`, `cas_path`, `hash_file`, `CAS_DIRNAME` via `__all__`.

### Step 2: Surface `cas_hash` on the `produces_check_passed` event (`artagents/core/task/events.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Update** `make_produces_check_passed_event` (`artagents/core/task/events.py:185`) to accept an optional `cas_hash: str | None = None`. When non-None, include `"cas_hash": cas_hash` (bare hex, no prefix) in the dict between `check_id` and `kind` so canonical-key ordering stays sorted.
2. **Confirm** `canonical_event_json` sorts keys — yes (used by the chain hash). When `cas_hash` is None the key is omitted, so legacy event payloads keep byte-identical canonical hashes and the existing chain replay tests stay green.

### Step 3: Wire CAS into the produces-check pass path (`artagents/core/task/gate.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Import** `from artagents.core.task.cas import intern, link_into_produces` in `gate.py`.
2. **Modify** `_run_inline_checks` (`artagents/core/task/gate.py:1110`). Inside the per-entry loop, after `result.ok` is true and before `append_event(... make_produces_check_passed_event ...)`:
   ```python
   cas_hash = None
   if artifact_path.is_file() or artifact_path.is_symlink():
       cas_entry, cas_hash = intern(decision.project_root, artifact_path)
       link_into_produces(cas_entry, artifact_path)
   ```
   `decision.project_root` is `<projects_root>/<slug>` (already populated; see line 1119).
3. **Pass** `cas_hash=cas_hash` into `make_produces_check_passed_event`.
4. **Skip** interning silently when the artifact is a directory or missing — the check already failed in that case (we run the intern only when `is_file()` or `is_symlink()`). `Path.is_file()` follows symlinks, so re-running over an already-interned symlink hits `intern`'s CAS short-circuit and is a no-op.

### Step 4: Make the hype-artifact mirror CAS-friendly (`artagents/core/project/run.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Edit** `mirror_hype_artifacts` (`artagents/core/project/run.py:236-255`) — change the single line `shutil.copy2(source_path, dest_path)` to `shutil.copy2(source_path, dest_path, follow_symlinks=False)`.
   - **Why:** when the hype run's source artifact is already a CAS symlink (from an earlier intern pass), `follow_symlinks=False` preserves the symlink at the dest. The parent task gate's `_run_inline_checks` then sees a symlink, `intern`'s short-circuit recognizes the CAS target and returns without any new write — preserving dedup. With the default `follow_symlinks=True`, the bytes would be copied out and re-interned, briefly doubling storage and producing a redundant CAS write.
   - **Behavior unchanged for regular-file sources:** `copy2` with `follow_symlinks=False` falls through to a normal copy when the source isn't a link, so no existing test changes behavior.
2. **No other edits to `run.py`** — the gate remains the single produces-check chokepoint.

### Step 5: Update repo guardrails (`.gitignore`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** `artagents/structure.py:14` — `TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS` only governs the in-repo `artagents/` package; project directories live external to the repo, so no `.cas` entry is needed there. The brief explicitly says "otherwise leave structure alone."
2. **Append** to `.gitignore`:
   ```
   # Per-project CAS (Phase 7) — guards in-repo test fixtures that materialize project dirs
   **/.cas/
   ```
   (No claim about `runs/` — CAS is per-project, not per-run.)

### Step 6: Add CAS unit tests (`tests/test_cas_intern.py`, `tests/test_cas_symlink.py`, `tests/test_cas_per_project.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`tests/test_cas_intern.py`** — exercise `intern` directly:
   - `test_intern_moves_regular_file_into_cas`: writes `data.bin` with `b"hello"`, calls `intern(project_dir, source)`, asserts `source` is gone, the CAS entry is a regular file at `project_dir/.cas/<sha256("hello")>`, and the returned hash is the bare hex of sha256("hello").
   - `test_intern_idempotent_discards_duplicate_source`: writes the same content to two different source paths, calls `intern` on each. Asserts exactly one file in `<project_dir>/.cas/`, both calls returned the same `(target, hash)`, and both source paths are gone.
   - `test_intern_short_circuits_on_existing_cas_symlink`: pre-creates a CAS entry, builds a relative symlink to it via `link_into_produces`, calls `intern` on the symlink. Asserts no new CAS entry, returned hash matches, and the symlink is untouched.
   - `test_intern_dereferences_outside_symlink_source`: makes `source` a symlink pointing to a regular file OUTSIDE `<project_dir>/.cas/`. Calls `intern`. Asserts the CAS entry is a regular file (`Path.is_symlink()` is False, `Path.is_file()` is True), the source symlink is gone, and the returned hash matches the dereferenced content.
2. **`tests/test_cas_symlink.py`** — full produces flow through the gate, mirroring the harness in `tests/test_task_inline_checks.py:113`:
   - Author a one-step plan with a `json_file` produces check, run `gate.gate_command`, write `out.json`, call `record_dispatch_complete`.
   - Assert `step_dir/out.json` is a symlink (`Path.is_symlink()`); `os.readlink` returns a relative path starting with `..` and containing `/.cas/`; the resolved target sits at `<project_dir>/.cas/<sha256>`; reading `out.json` returns the original JSON content.
   - Assert the `produces_check_passed` event in `events.jsonl` carries a `cas_hash` field equal to the CAS entry filename (bare hex).
3. **`tests/test_cas_per_project.py`** — per-project scope:
   - Create two projects (`alpha`, `beta`) under the same `tmp_projects_root`.
   - Drive identical produces flow in both with identical content.
   - Assert `<root>/alpha/.cas/<hash>` AND `<root>/beta/.cas/<hash>` both exist as separate inodes (`Path.stat().st_ino` differs).
   - Assert no `<root>/.cas/` directory exists (no shared CAS leaked).

### Step 7: Run the test suite
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_cas_intern.py tests/test_cas_symlink.py tests/test_cas_per_project.py -x -q` first.
2. **Run** `pytest tests/test_task_inline_checks.py tests/test_task_kernel_gate.py tests/test_project_runs.py tests/test_banodoco_worker.py -q` to catch regressions in the immediate Phase 1–3 surface and the hype path that Step 4 touches.
3. **Run** `pytest tests/ -q` for the full additive guarantee.

## Execution Order
1. Land `cas.py` (Step 1) and the optional `cas_hash` plumbing on the event factory (Step 2) — both are pure additions, no behavior change yet.
2. Wire the gate hook (Step 3) — this flips the produces flow from regular files to symlinks.
3. Add the `follow_symlinks=False` defensive edit to `mirror_hype_artifacts` (Step 4) so the hype mirror preserves CAS symlinks instead of dereferencing them.
4. Add the gitignore entry (Step 5) so any in-repo `.cas/` fixtures stay un-staged.
5. Add tests (Step 6).
6. Validate (Step 7).

## Validation Order
1. Targeted CAS tests first (`test_cas_intern`, `test_cas_symlink`, `test_cas_per_project`).
2. Phase 1–3 regression sweep plus the hype path (`test_task_kernel_gate`, `test_task_inline_checks`, `test_project_runs`, `test_banodoco_worker`).
3. Full `pytest tests/`.

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

214	def redact_cli_args(argv: Iterable[str]) -> list[str]:
215	    redacted: list[str] = []
216	    hide_next = False
217	    for raw in argv:
218	        arg = str(raw)
219	        if hide_next:
220	            redacted.append("<redacted>")
221	            hide_next = False
222	            continue
223	        if "=" in arg:
224	            key, _value = arg.split("=", 1)
225	            if _is_sensitive_key(key):
226	                redacted.append(f"{key}=<redacted>")
227	                continue
228	        if _is_sensitive_key(arg):
229	            redacted.append(arg)
230	            hide_next = True
231	            continue
232	        redacted.append(arg)
233	    return redacted

253	        shutil.copy2(source_path, dest_path)
254	        mirrored[key] = {"path": str(dest_path), "source_path": str(source_path)}
255	    return mirrored

115	def step_dir_for_path(

124	    validate_project_slug(slug)

131	        raise TaskPlanError("step_dir_for_path: iteration and item_id are mutually exclusive")
132	    base = project_dir(slug, root=root) / "runs" / run_id / "steps"
133	    for segment in plan_step_path:

135	    if iteration is not None:

142	    return base
